are given by:

\bullet

Reward function
\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
for
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
.
We set the reward to be
0
for all states
\phi\notin{\mathcal{I}}
. Furthermore, for
\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}
, we set the reward to be
0
for actions that would not have been chosen by
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}
. On the remaining
(\phi,a)
tuples, we set a non-zero reward given by:

\displaystyle\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi,{a})=\frac{\varepsilon}{2H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}\cdot\mathbb{I}\{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}},{a}={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi)\},

where
h^{*}
and
{\mathcal{I}}
are the maximizer in
Definition
3.1
. The key intuition in the above choice of reward function is to ensure that only those states-action contribute to non-zero rewards for which
\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}
and
a={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi)
; Hence, in order to receive a non-zero return, any agent in this
MDP
needs to first find states in
{\mathcal{I}}
and then play action given by
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}
on them. The denominator just consists of additional normalizing factors to ensure that the value is bounded by
\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{2H}}
.

\bullet

Reward function
\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
for
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
.
The reward function is similar to
\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
, but with the negative sign. In particular, we define

\displaystyle\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\phi,{a})=-\frac{\varepsilon}{2H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}\cdot\mathbb{I}\{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}},{a}={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi)\}.

Definition C.1

(Aggregated MDP)
.

We define aggregated MDPs
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=(\Phi,\mathcal{A},\bar{T},\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},H,\bar{\rho})
and
\bar{M}^{(2)}=(\Phi,\mathcal{A},\bar{T},\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},H,\bar{\rho})
as follows: transition model
\bar{T}
is defined as
\bar{T}(\phi\mid\phi,{a})\vcentcolon={}\bar{T}(\phi\mid\phi,{a};\bar{M})
, where
\bar{T}(\phi\mid\phi,{a};\bar{M})
is given in
(
3
)
, reward functions are defined as

\displaystyle\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi,{a})=\frac{\varepsilon}{2H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}\cdot\mathbb{I}\{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}},{a}={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi)\},

and,

\displaystyle\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\phi,{a})=-\frac{\varepsilon}{2H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}\cdot\mathbb{I}\{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}},{a}={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi)\}.

Lemma 1
.

The value functions of
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
satisfies that for any policy
\pi
and
\phi\in\Phi
,

V^{\pi}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=-V^{\pi}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(@)}).

Proof of
Lemma
1
.

This lemma is easy to see after noticing that the transitions of
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
are the same, while the reward functions of
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
are of opposite signs.

\blacksquare

Lemma 2
.

The value functions of
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
satisfies that

V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\bar{\rho};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=\frac{\varepsilon}{2H},\quad\text{and }V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\bar{\rho};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})=-\frac{\varepsilon}{2H}.

Additionally, for each
\phi\in\Phi
,

0\leq V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\leq\frac{1}{2H},\qquad-\frac{1}{2H}\leq V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\leq 0.

(11)

Proof of
Lemma
2
.

According to
Lemma
1
, we only need to prove results for
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
. First of all, we can write the value functions as weighted averages of rewards with occupancy-measure-weights:

\displaystyle V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\bar{\rho};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}r^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi))d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})=\frac{\varepsilon}{2H}.

(12)

Next, We let
{\mathcal{I}}
to be the set which attains the second maximum in
(
2
)
of the definition of
\mathsf{C}_{\varepsilon}(M,\mu)
. Then
{\mathcal{I}}\subset\Phi_{h}
for some
h\in[H-1]
, and it also satisfies
\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})\geq\varepsilon
, which implies that

0\leq\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi,{a})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2H\cdot\varepsilon}\leq\frac{1}{2H},\quad\forall\phi\in\Phi_{h},a\in\mathcal{A}\qquad\text{and }\qquad\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi,{a})=0,\quad\forall\phi\not\in\Phi_{h},{a}\in\mathcal{A}.

Hence for any
\phi\in\Phi
, the sum of rewards along any trajectory which starts from
\phi
is always between
0
and
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2H}}
in
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
. Therefore we get
0\leq V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2H}}
for any
\phi\in\Phi
.

\blacksquare

C.3
Construction of Latent-State MDPs and
OPE
Problems

Based on
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
, we next construct two MDPs
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
which will be used as latent-state dynamics for rich observation MDPs that we construct in the next section. For
i\in\{1,2\}
, we define

\displaystyle M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}

\displaystyle=\text{MDP}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{A},T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},r^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},H,\rho),

where

\bullet

State space
\bm{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}
is defined such that
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\cup_{h=1}^{H}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{h}
where, for each
h\in[H]
, in addition to the states in
\mathcal{Z}_{h}
, the set
\mathcal{Z}_{h}^{\prime}
contains three additional states
\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}
for all
h\in[H]
. Formally,
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{h}\vcentcolon={}\mathcal{Z}_{h}\cup\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}
.

The roles of
u_{h},v_{h}
and
w_{h}
is to ensure that for every aggregated state
\phi\in\Phi
, each state
z\in\phi
has the same value functione; How we achieve this will become clear later when we define the transition model
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
.

\bullet

Initial distribution
\bm{\rho}
is the same as the initial distribution in
M
(the original
MDP
that was used in the construction of
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
).

\bullet

Reward function
r^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
is set as

\displaystyle r^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z},{a})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}(\zeta({z}),{a})&\text{if}\quad{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h},\\
1&\text{if}\quad{z}={u}_{h},\\
-1&\text{if}\quad{z}={v}_{h},\\
0&\text{if}\quad{z}={w}_{h}.\\
\end{cases}

for all
h\in[H-1]
, where
\zeta({z})
is defined in
(
7
)
. In particular, we use the same reward in
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
as in
\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
for the (old) states
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, and define new rewards for (newly added) states
{u}_{h},{v}_{h}
and
{w}_{h}
. By definition, the reward
r^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime},a)=r^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime\prime},{a})
whenever
{z}^{\prime}
and
{z}^{\prime\prime}
belong the same aggregated state
\phi
, for all
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
.

\bullet

Transition model
\bm{T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}}
.
The transitions are defined such that
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})
is proportional to
T({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})
for tuples
({z},{a},{z}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{Z}_{h}
that corresponds to transitions amongst (old) states that were also present in
M
, and the remaining probability mass is redirected it to new states
\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
. Formally, for
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and action
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
, we set

\displaystyle T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=\begin{cases}\penalty\ \left(1-\frac{2}{H}\right)T({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})&\text{if}\quad{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1},\\
\penalty\ [\Delta T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}](z)+\frac{1}{H}&\text{if}\quad{z}^{\prime}={u}_{h+1},\\
\penalty\ -[\Delta T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}](z)+\frac{1}{H}&\text{if}\quad{z}^{\prime}={v}_{h+1},\\
\penalty\ 0&\text{if}\quad{z}^{\prime}={w}_{h+1},\end{cases}

(13)

where we defined

\displaystyle[\Delta T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}](z)\vcentcolon={}\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\zeta({z}),{a})V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi^{\prime};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})-\left(1-\frac{2}{H}\right)\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}}T({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}^{\prime});\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})\right).

(14)

Furthermore, for any
{z}\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{w},{w}_{h}\}
and
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
,
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
transits to
w_{h+1}
with probability
1
, i.e.

\displaystyle T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=\begin{cases}\penalty\ 1&\text{if}\quad{z}^{\prime}={w}_{h+1}\\
\penalty\ 0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(15)

Intuitively, the above transitions imply that states
\{{w}_{h}\}_{h\in[H-1]}
act as terminal states.

We further define distribution
\mu_{h}^{\prime}
over
\mathcal{Z}_{h}^{\prime}
of layer
h\in[H-1]
as follows:

\mu^{\prime}_{h}({z})=\begin{cases}\frac{\mu_{h}({z})}{2}&\text{if}\quad{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h},\\
\frac{1}{6}&\text{if}\quad{z}\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}.\end{cases}

(16)

Additionally, to be formal, we define
{\pi_{b}}({z})={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})\equiv a_{0}
(an arbitrary fixed state in
\mathcal{A}
) for any
{z}\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}
, and the
OPE
problems
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
as

\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu^{\prime},\mathcal{F}),\quad\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu^{\prime},\mathcal{F}),

(17)

where
\mathcal{F}\vcentcolon={}\{Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\}
with
Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
are state-action value functions of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
under policy
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}
.
Before we proceed, we note the following technical lemma.

Lemma 3
.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
,
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
constructed via
(
13
)
and
(
15
)
above is a valid transition model.

Proof of
Lemma
3
.

We first show that
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})>0
for all
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
and
{z},{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{h+1}
. This is trivial for
{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
or when
{z}^{\prime}={w}_{h+1}
. We next show that the same holds when
{z}^{\prime}\in\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}
.

Note that due to
Lemma
2
, we have that
\lvert V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2H}
for all
\phi\in\Phi
. Plugging this in
(
14
)
, and using Triangle inequality, we get that

\displaystyle\left\lvert[\Delta T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}](z)\right\rvert\leq\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}\left\lvert V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2H}.

Using this in
(
13
)
, we immediately get that
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})>0
for any
\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, it is easy to check that for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{h}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{h+1}}T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=1.

Thus,
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
is a valid transition model.

\blacksquare

We note the following technical lemma, which will be used in the rest of the analysis.

Lemma 4
.

We have the following properties of value functions, state-action value functions and occupancy measures of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
:

(a)

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, and for any
\phi\in\Phi
,
{z}\in\phi
, and
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

Q^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{a};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})=Q^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}),{a};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}).

(b)

Corresponding to the initial distribution
\rho
, the expected values satisfy

V^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\rho;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})-V^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\rho;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})=\frac{\varepsilon}{H}.

(c)

For any
h\in[H-1]
, latent state
\phi\in\Phi_{h}
, action
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
and latent state
{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z}\mid{z}\in\phi,{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z}\mid{z}\in\phi,{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a}).

(d)

For each
i\in\{1,2\}
, for any
h\in[H-1]
and
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
, and policy
\pi
,

\frac{1}{16}d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)\leq d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})\leq d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M).

Proof of
Lemma
4
.
Observe that, by construction, we have for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h},{a})=1,\qquad Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({v}_{h},{a})=-1,\text{and}\qquad Q^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({w}_{h},{a})=0,

for any
h
and
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
.

Proof of

(
a
)

:

The proof follows via downward induction from
h=H
to
h=1
. First note that for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{H}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we have
Q^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{a};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})=0=Q^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}),{a};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})
; Thus the base case is satisfied. For the induction hypotesis, assume that the desired claim holds for
h+1
. Thus, for layer
h
, using Bellman equation for the policy
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359ptQ^{\pi}({z},{a};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})

\displaystyle=r^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z},{a})+T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h+1}\mid{z},{a})-T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({v}_{h+1}\mid{z},{a})+\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}}T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})Q^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}^{\prime},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}^{\prime});M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})

\displaystyle=\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}(\zeta({z}),{a})+T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h+1}\mid{z},{a})-T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({v}_{h+1}\mid{z},{a})+\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}}T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}^{\prime});\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})

\displaystyle=\bar{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}(\zeta({z}),{a})+\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\zeta({z}),{a})V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi^{\prime};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})

\displaystyle=Q^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}),{a};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}),

where in the last line, we used Bellman equation for policy
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}
under the
MDP

\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
. The above completes the induction step, thus showing that the claim holds for all
h\in[H]
.

Proof of

(
b
)

:

Using part-

(
a
)

above, we note that

\displaystyle V^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=Q^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z});M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=Q^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}),{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z}));\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z});\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}).

Similarly, we also get that
V^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})=V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta({z});\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})
. The desired bound follows by noting that
(
12
)
implies

V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\bar{\rho};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})-V^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\bar{\rho};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})=\frac{\varepsilon}{H},

which implies

V^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\rho;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})-V^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\rho;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})=\frac{\varepsilon}{H}.

Proof of

(
c
)

:

First note that whenever
{z}^{\prime}\not\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
, we always have
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})
according to the definition of
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
. We next show that the same holds for
{z}^{\prime}={u}_{h}
. We only need to verify
\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})(T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},{a})-T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},{a}))=0
.

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt\quad\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})(T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},{a})-T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},{a}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})\left(\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\zeta({z}),{a})\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi^{\prime})-\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}}T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\zeta({z}^{\prime}))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\phi,{a})\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi^{\prime})-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\zeta({z}^{\prime}))\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\phi,{a})\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi^{\prime})-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi^{\prime})\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\phi,{a})\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi^{\prime})-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}}\bar{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\phi^{\prime})\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})\bar{T}(\phi^{\prime}\mid\phi,{a})

\displaystyle=0,

where the first equation uses the definition of
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
in
(
13
)
and
Lemma
1
, and the last equation follows from the definition of
\bar{T}
in
(
3
)
. Hence

(
c
)

is verified for
{z}^{\prime}={u}_{h}
. The proof for
{z}^{\prime}={v}_{h}
follows similarly.

Proof of

(
d
)

:

We only prove the result for
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
; the proof for
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
follows similarly. In fact, we show a slightly stronger result that for all
h\in[H]
and
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{H-2}{H}\right)^{h-1}d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)\leq d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\leq d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M).

(18)

The proof follows via induction over
h
. For the base case, note that for
h=1
, by definition, we have
d_{1}^{\pi}({z};M)=\rho({z})=d_{1}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})
for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}
, which implies
(
18
)
.

For the induction step, suppose
(
18
)
holds for a certain
h\leq H-1
. For the upper bound, note that any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}
satisfies

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}d_{h}^{\pi}({z}^{\prime};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime})),

which combined with the upper bound in
(
18
)
implies

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}d_{h}^{\pi}({z}^{\prime};M)T({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime}))=d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z};M).

For the lower bound, recall from the definition of
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
, which implies that

T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime}))=\frac{H-2}{H}T({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime})).

Using the above with the lower bound in
(
18
)
, we get that

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}^{\prime}}d_{h}^{\pi}({z}^{\prime};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{H-2}{H}\right)^{h-1}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}d_{h}^{\pi}({z}^{\prime};M)\cdot\frac{H-2}{H}T({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime}))=\left(\frac{H-2}{H}\right)^{h}d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M).

The two bounds above imply the
(
18
)
also holds for
h+1
. This completes the induction step.

\blacksquare

This lemma has two direct corollaries:

Corollary 1
.

The concentratbility coefficients
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mu^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\mu^{\prime})
of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
, respectively, satisfy that

\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mu^{\prime})\leq 6\mathsf{C}(M,\mu,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\mu^{\prime})\leq 6\mathsf{C}(M,\mu,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}).

Proof of
Corollary
1
.

This corollary directly follows from
Definition
16
and
Lemma
4

(
d
)

.

\blacksquare

Corollary 2
.

For any two policies
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
and any
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}^{\prime}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\leq 48\cdot\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}

Proof of
Corollary
2
.

First of all, for those
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
h\in[H-1]
,
Lemma
4

(
d
)

indicates that

\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\leq 16\cdot\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}\leq 48\cdot\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}.

Next, we verify cases where

{z}\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
. Notice that the transition model of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
gives that

d_{h}^{\pi}({w}_{h};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})=d_{h-1}^{\pi}({u}_{h};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})+d_{h-1}^{\pi}({v}_{h-1};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}),

we only need to verify that for any
{z}\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
, we have

\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\leq 48\cdot\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}.

Without loss of generality, we only verify for
{z}={u}_{h}
. We write

d_{h}^{\pi}({u}_{h};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})=\sum_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h-1}}T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},\pi({z}))d_{h-1}^{\pi}({z}).

According to the transition model of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
, we have for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h-1}
and any
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
,

T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},{a})\in\left[\frac{1}{2H},\frac{3}{2H}\right],

which indicates that for any policy
\pi,\pi^{\prime}
, we have

\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h-1}}\frac{T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},\pi({z}))}{T^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}({u}_{h}\mid{z},\pi^{\prime}({z}))}\in[1,3].

Therefore, we have

\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({u}_{h};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({u}_{h};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\leq 3\cdot\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h-1}}\frac{d_{h-1}^{\pi}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}{d_{h-1}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\leq 48\cdot\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)},

where the last inequality follows from
Lemma
4

(
d
)

.

\blacksquare

C.4
Construction of the Class
\mathfrak{G}
of Offline Policy Evaluation Problems

In this section, we construct the class
\mathfrak{G}
of
OPE
problems that are used in
Theorem
3.1
. The corresponding MDPs in
\mathfrak{G}
are block MDPs based on
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
(constructed in the previous section), and certain decoder functions. The organization of this section is as follows:

\bullet

In
Appendix
C.4.1
, we provide a general procedure to lift an
OPE
problem
\textsc{OPE}(M,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu,\mathcal{F})
over state space
\mathcal{Z}
into a block
OPE
problem
(\check{M},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}},\check{\mu},\check{\mathcal{F}})
with rich-observations in a set
\mathcal{X}
and latent states in
\mathcal{Z}
, given a decoder function
\psi
.

\bullet

Then, in
Appendix
C.4.2
, we first provide a class
\Psi
of decoder function and use the above procedure to construct the family
\mathfrak{G}
of offline RL problems.

C.4.1
Lifting from
OPE
Problems to Block
OPE
Problems

In this section, we will discuss how to lift a normal
OPE
problem
\textsc{OPE}(M,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu,\mathcal{F})
(
\mathcal{F}
satisfies
Q
-realizability) into a block
4
4

4

Throughout this section, we use a ”check” over the variables, e.g. in
\check{M}
,
\check{r}
, etc., to signify that they correspond to block
MDP
s.

OPE
problem
\textsc{OPE}(\check{M},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}},\check{\mu},\check{\mathcal{F}})
where
\check{\mathcal{F}}
satisfies
(Q,W)
-realizability.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=\bigcup_{h\in[H-1]}\mathcal{Z}_{h}
be the state space of
MDP

M
, and we fix
\mathcal{X}=\{\mathcal{X}({z})\}_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}}
to be a family of disjoint sets that denote rich-observations corresponding to latent states
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Furthermore, let
\mathcal{X}_{h}=\{\mathcal{X}({z})\}_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}
. Then, for
M=\textsc{MDP}(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A},T,r,H,\rho)
, we can define a block
MDP

\displaystyle\check{M}=\text{MDP}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},\check{T},\check{R},H,\check{\rho})

with latent state
\mathcal{Z}
and rich observations in
\mathcal{X}
, where

•

State space
:
\mathcal{X}
consists of rich-observations corresponding to latent states. We assume that the state space
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup_{h\in[H-1]}\mathcal{X}_{h}
is layered.

•

Transition Model
depends only on the latent transition model
T
. In particular, for any
h\in[H-1]
,
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
corresponding to
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
, and
{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}({z}^{\prime})
corresponding to
{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}
, we have

\displaystyle\check{T}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\vcentcolon={}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{X}({z}^{\prime})|}\cdot T({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a}).

•

Rewards
: For any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, and
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
,
\check{R}({x},{a})
is a
\{-1,1\}
-valued random variable with expected value
r({z},{a})
.

•

Initial distribution
:
\check{\rho}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}_{1})
is defined such that for any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}
,

\check{\rho}({x})\vcentcolon={}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}\cdot\rho({z})

(19)

where
{z}
is such that
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
.

In particular, corresponding to a latent state
{z}
, the observations are sampled from
\text{Uniform}(\mathcal{X}(z))
. In order to construct respective offline RL problems on the
MDP

\check{M}
, we also lift the offline data distribution
\mu
to
\check{\mu}
, and offline policy
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}
to
{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}
as follows:

•

Offline distribution
For any
h\in[H-1]
, we define
\check{\mu}_{h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A})
such that for any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\check{\mu}_{h}({x},{a})\vcentcolon={}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}\cdot\mu_{h}({z},{a}),

where
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
is such that
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
.

•

Evaluation policy

{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathcal{A}
is defined such that for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,
{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x})\vcentcolon={}{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})
where
{z}
is such that
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
.

•

Function class

\check{\mathcal{F}}
consists of tuples
(\check{f},\check{W})
, where each
f\in\mathcal{F}
generates a tuple
(\check{f},\check{W})
with
\check{f}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{R}
defined as
\check{f}({x},{a})=f({z},{a})
for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
and
\check{W}:[H-1]\to\mathbb{R}
is defined as
\check{W}[H-1]=\sum_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\check{\mu}_{h}({x},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}))\check{f}({x},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({a}))
.

The following lemma indicates that
\check{f}
is the state-action value function of block
MDP

\check{M}
as long as
f
is the state-action value function of
MDP

M
.

Lemma 5
.

For any
h\in[H]
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
,
x\in\mathcal{X}({z})
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

Q_{h}^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}}({x},{a};\check{M})=Q_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{a};M).

Proof of
Lemma
5
.

We prove this equation by induction from
h=H
to
h=1
. When
h=H
, we have
Q_{H}^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}}({x},{a};\check{M})=Q_{H}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{a};M)
for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{H}
,
{z}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
and
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
. Next, suppose
Q_{h+1}^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}}({x},{a};\check{M})=Q_{h+1}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{a};M)
holds for
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
.
According to the Bellman equation and definitions of
\check{T},\check{R}
, for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x},{a};\check{M})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\check{R}({x},{a})]+\sum_{{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}}\check{T}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})Q_{h+1}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}^{\prime},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}^{\prime});\check{M})

\displaystyle=r({z},{a})+\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}}Q_{h+1}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}^{\prime},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}^{\prime}))\sum_{{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}({z}^{\prime})}\check{T}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})

\displaystyle=r({z},{a})+\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}}Q_{h+1}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}^{\prime},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}^{\prime});M)\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}T({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})

\displaystyle=Q_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{a};M),

where we use the induction hypothesis and the fact that
{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}^{\prime})={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})
for
{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}({z}^{\prime})
in the second equation.

\blacksquare

Lemma 6
.

For any
h\in[H-1]
,

\sum_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\check{\mu}_{h}({x},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}))Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x});\check{M})=\sum_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\mu_{h}({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}))Q_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x});M).

Proof of
Lemma
6
.

We first notice that
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
, we have
{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x})={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})
. Hence according to
Lemma
5
we only need to verify that, for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
,

\sum_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})}\check{\mu}_{h}({x},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}))=\mu_{h}({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})),

which is given by definition
\check{\mu}_{h}({x},{a})\vcentcolon={}\frac{\mu_{h}({z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 7
.

For any policy
\pi
over
MDP

M
, let policy
\check{\pi}
over
\check{M}
to be
\check{\pi}({x})=\pi({z})
for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
. Then we have for any
h\in[H]
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
,

d_{h}^{\check{\pi}}({x};\check{M})=\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}.

(20)

Proof of
Lemma
7
.

We will prove via induction on the layer of
{x}
. For
{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{1}
,
(
20
)
holds acording to the definition of initial distribution
\check{\rho}
. The induction from layer
h
to layer
h+1
can be achieved by

d_{h+1}^{\check{\pi}}({x};\check{M})=\sum_{{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}d_{h}^{\check{\pi}}({x}^{\prime};\check{M})\check{T}({x}\mid{x}^{\prime},\check{\pi}({x}^{\prime}))=\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}d_{h}^{\pi}({z}^{\prime};M)\frac{T({z}\mid{z}^{\prime},\pi({z}^{\prime}))}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}=\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}

for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
and
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}
.
\blacksquare

The above lemma has the following two corollaries:

Corollary 3
.

The concentrability coefficient
\mathsf{C}(\check{M},\mu,{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}})=\mathsf{C}(M,\mu,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}})
.

Proof of
Corollary
3
.

According to
Lemma
7
, we have for any
h\in[H-1]
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
,

\frac{d_{h}^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}}({x};\check{M})}{\check{\mu}({x})}=\frac{\nicefrac{{d_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z};M)}}{{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}}}{\nicefrac{{\mu({z})}}{{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}}}=\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z};M)}{\mu({z})}.

Taking the supremum over all
h\in[H-1]
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
, we get

\mathsf{C}(\check{M},\mu,{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}})=\sup_{h\in[H-1]}\sup_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}}({x};\check{M})}{\check{\mu}({x})}=\sup_{h\in[H-1]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z};M)}{\mu({z})}=\mathsf{C}(M,\mu,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}).

\blacksquare

Corollary 4
.

For any two policies
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
, let policy
\check{\pi}
and
\check{\pi}^{\prime}
over
\check{M}
be defined such that
\check{\pi}({x})=\pi({z})
and
\check{\pi}^{\prime}({x})=\pi^{\prime}({z})
for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
. Then, we have

\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\check{\pi}}({x};\check{M})}{d_{h}^{\check{\pi}^{\prime}}({x};\check{M})}=\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}.

Proof of
Corollary
4
.

According to
Lemma
7
, we have for any
h\in[H]
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
,

\frac{d_{h}^{\check{\pi}}({x};\check{M})}{d_{h}^{\check{\pi}^{\prime}}({x};\check{M})}=\frac{\nicefrac{{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}}{{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}}}{\nicefrac{{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}}{{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}}}=\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}.

Taking the supremum over all
h\in[H]
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
, we get

\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\check{\pi}}({x};\check{M})}{d_{h}^{\check{\pi}^{\prime}}({x};\check{M})}=\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi^{\prime}}({z};M)}.

\blacksquare

C.4.2
Construction of the family of offline RL problems

We will construct two
OPE
families
\mathfrak{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
by lifting
OPE
problems
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
defined in
(
17
)
into Block
OPE
problems. Each Block
OPE
problem has the same observation space but a different emission distributions. Furthermore, each of these Block
OPE
problem has latent state space
\mathcal{Z}
and is based on the same aggregation scheme
\Phi
.

let
\{\mathcal{X}(\phi)\}_{\phi\in\mathcal{Z}}
be a family of disjoint sets that denote rich-observations corresponding to aggregated states states
\phi\in\Phi
such that

|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|\gtrsim\frac{|\phi|^{3}H^{8}\cdot\sup_{h\in H}|\Phi_{h}|\cdot\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}}.

(21)

The observation space for all the Block-MDPs is given by
\mathcal{X}=\cup_{h=1}^{H}\mathcal{X}_{h}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{h}={\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}}\cup\left(\cup_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}\mathcal{X}(\phi)\right).

The Block-MDPs that we construct next will different in terms of which observations from
\mathcal{X}(\phi)
will be assigned to latent states
{z}\in\phi
. To make this explicit, we rely on decoder functions that map

\psi:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathcal{Z}
. Without loss of generality, assume that all decoders that we will consider satisfy
\psi({u}_{h})={u}_{h}
,
\psi({v}_{h})={v}_{h}
and
\psi({w}_{h})={w}_{h}
for all
h\in[H]
. Additionally, given a decoder
\psi
, we define the set
\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})=\{{x}\in\mathcal{X}\mid\psi({x})={z}\}
. We finally define the set
\Psi
as the set of all possible decoders which ensure that for any
\phi\in\Phi
, each latent state
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
gets the same number of observations from
\mathcal{Z}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\Psi=\left\{\psi:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathcal{Z}\mid{}\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})\subseteq\mathcal{X}\penalty\ \text{and}\penalty\ \lvert\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})\rvert=\frac{\lvert\mathcal{X}(\phi)\rvert}{\lvert\phi\rvert}\quad\forall\phi\in\Phi,\forall{z}\in\phi\right\}.

Offline Policy Evaluation (OPE) Problem given
\psi
.

Given a decoder
\psi
, and the above notation, we will lift
OPE
problem
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},\mu^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
into
OPE
problems
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi}=\textsc{OPE}(\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}},\check{\mu}_{\psi}^{\prime},\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi})
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{\psi}=\textsc{OPE}(\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{\psi},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}},\check{\mu}_{\psi}^{\prime},\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi})
using the recipe in
Appendix
C.4.1
, with
\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}
satisfies

(Q^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi}),W^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi}^{\prime},\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi}))\in\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}\quad\text{and}\quad(Q^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{\psi}),W^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi}^{\prime},\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{\psi}))\in\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi},

where the
W
function is defined in
Definition
B.3
.

Family of
OPE
problems.

We finally define the family
\mathfrak{G}
for
OPE
problems as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}=\bigcup_{\psi\in\Psi}\{\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{\psi}\}.

We note the following useful technical lemma.

Lemma 8
.

For any
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi
, we have that
\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}=\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi^{\prime}}
.

Proof of
Lemma
8
.

We will only prove the results for
\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
. To verify this, we only need to prove that for any
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi
, we have for any
1\leq h\leq H
,

Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{M}_{\psi^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\quad\text{and}\quad W^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(h;\check{\mu}_{\psi}^{\prime},\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi}))=W^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(h;\check{\mu}_{\psi}^{\prime},\check{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\psi^{\prime}})).

The second equation directly follows from
Lemma
6
. In the next, we will verify the first equation.
Lemma
5
gives that for any
\phi\in\Phi_{h}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi)
and
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x},{a};\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=Q_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\psi({x}),{a};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}).

Next,
Lemma
4

(
a
)

indicates that

Q_{h}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\psi({x}),{a};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=Q_{h}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\zeta(\psi({x})),{a};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}).

Notice that for every
{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi)
, we have
\zeta(\psi({x}))=\phi
for any
\psi\in\Psi
. Hence for any
\psi\in\Psi
, we have
Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x},{a};\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})=Q_{h}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi,{a};\bar{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})
for every
\phi\in\Phi
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi)
, which is independent to
\psi
.

\blacksquare

In the following, we let
\mathcal{F}=\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}
for some
\psi\in\Psi
. Then we have for any
\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi
,
\mathcal{F}=\check{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi^{\prime}}
as well.

C.5
Proof of
Theorem
3.1

After constructing the class
\mathfrak{G}
, we have finished the construction step. In the rest of the section we will prove the theorem by analyzing properties of
OPE
problems in
\mathfrak{G}
. In fact, we will prove the following stronger results than
Theorem
3.1
:

Theorem C.1
.

Class
\mathfrak{G}
satisfies: for every
OPE
problem
\mathfrak{g}=\textsc{OPE}\left(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}\right)
in
\mathfrak{G}
,

(a)

The function class
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
satisfies
\lvert\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}\rvert=2
and

(Q^{{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}),W^{{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})};M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}))\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}.

(b)

The concentrability coefficients satisfy
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})})\leq 6\mathsf{C}(M,\mu,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}})
;

Furthermore, for
N=o(\nicefrac{{H\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)}}{{\varepsilon}})
and any algorithm which takes
\mathcal{D}=\cup_{h=1}^{H}\mathcal{D}_{h}
as input and output the evaluation of value function
\widehat{V}
, there must exist some
\mathfrak{g}=\textsc{OPE}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})})\in\mathfrak{G}
such that the algorithm fails to output
\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{H}}
-accurate evaluation with probability at least
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
, if the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{h}=\{({x}_{h},{a}_{h},r_{h},{x}_{h+1}^{\prime})\}
consists of
N
i.i.d. samples collected where
({x}_{h},{a}_{h})\sim\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
and
r_{h},{x}_{h+1}^{\prime}
are collected according to the reward function and transition model of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Proposition 2
.

The aggregated concentrability coefficient of
OPE
problems
\mathfrak{g}=\textsc{OPE}(\check{M},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}},\check{\mu}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})\in\mathfrak{G}
satisfies
\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(\check{M},\check{\Phi},\check{\mu}^{\prime})=\Theta(\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu))
where the aggregation scheme
\check{\Phi}
is defined over
\mathcal{X}
such that
\check{\Phi}_{h}=\{\mathcal{X}(\phi):\phi\in\Phi_{h}\}\cup\{\{{u}_{h}\},\{{v}_{h}\},\{{w}_{h}\}\}
.

C.5.1
Useful Technical Lemmas for the Proof of
Theorem
C.1

In this subsection we provide several useful technical lemmas for proof of
Theorem
C.1
.

To begin with, we denote

\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x})\vcentcolon={}\begin{cases}\nicefrac{{\mu({z})}}{{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})|}}&\quad\forall{z}\in\mathcal{Z},{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z}),\\
0&\quad\forall{x}\in\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}\end{cases}.

Since the transition of
{u}_{h},{v}_{h},{w}_{h}
are already known, and
\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x})\geq\check{\mu}^{\prime}_{\psi}({x})
for all
{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})
and
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
, in the following we only need to prove the results for
OPE
problems
\mathfrak{g}=\textsc{OPE}(\check{M},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}},\check{\mu},\mathcal{F})
.

In the following, we use
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}(\cdot;\check{\mu},\check{M})
where
\check{M}=\textsc{MDP}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},H,\check{T},\check{R},\check{\rho})
to denote the law of
n
tuples of
({x},{a},r,{x}^{\prime})
jointly, where each tuple is i.i.d. collecting as follows: first sample
({x},{a})\sim\check{\mu}_{h}
, then sample
r\sim\check{R}(\cdot\mid{x},{a}),{x}^{\prime}\sim\check{T}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})
. Let

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{h,n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{h,n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}).

(22)

Furthermore, for any
\check{M}
, let

\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu},\check{M})\vcentcolon={}\bigotimes_{h=1}^{H-1}\mathbb{P}_{h,n}(\cdot;\check{\mu},\check{M})
, and using this notation, define

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}).

(23)

Additionally, since the state space
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\dots\cup\mathcal{S}_{H}
is layered, we get that
\psi
can be separated across layers, and thus the above definitions imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\bigotimes_{h=1}^{H-1}\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=\bigotimes_{h=1}^{H-1}\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}.

(24)

We have the following inequality for TV distance between product measures.

Lemma 9

(
Polyanskiy and Wu 2014
, I.33(b))
.

For distributions
\mathbb{P}_{1},\cdots,\mathbb{P}_{H}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathbb{Q}_{H}
, we have

D_{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\bigotimes_{h=1}^{H-1}\mathbb{P}_{h},\bigotimes_{h=1}^{H-1}\mathbb{Q}_{h}\right)\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{h},\mathbb{Q}_{h}\right).

To prove this theorem, we first show the following lemma.

Lemma 10
.

For any algorithm which takes
D_{h,n}=\{({x}_{h,i},{a}_{h,i},r_{h,i},{x}_{h,i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}
where
h\in[H-1]
as input and returns a value
\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})
(where we use
D_{1:H-1,n}
to denote
D_{1,n},\cdots,D_{H-1,n}
), it must satisfy

\sup_{\psi\in\Psi,i\in\{1,2\}}\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\left[\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V(\check{\rho};\check{M})\right|\right]\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{4H}\cdot\left(1-\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\right).

Proof of
Lemma
10
.

Lemma
8
gives that for any
\check{M},\check{M}^{\prime}\in\{\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}:\psi\in\Psi\}
, we have
Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{M})=Q_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\cdot;\check{M}^{\prime})
, which implies that MDPs in
\{\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}:\psi\in\Psi\}
share the same value function. Hence we have

V_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\check{\rho};\check{M})=V_{h}^{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\check{\rho};\check{M}^{\prime}),\quad\forall M,M^{\prime}\in\{\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}:\psi\in\Psi\}.

In the following, we denote the above quantity to be
V^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\check{\rho})
. Similarly, we denote
V^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\check{\rho})
to be the counterpart for MDPs in
\{\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}:\psi\in\Psi\}
.

For any dataset
D_{1:H-1,n}
, we use
\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})
to denote the following random variable:

\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})=\mathbb{I}\left\{\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})\leq\frac{V^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\check{\rho})+V^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\check{\rho})}{2}\right\}\in\{0,1\},

Then for any
\psi\in\Psi
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V(\check{\rho};\check{M})\right|

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}\left[\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})\cdot\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\check{\rho})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}\left[\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})\cdot\left|\frac{V^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\check{\rho})+V^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\check{\rho})}{2}-V^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\check{\rho})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}(\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})=1)\cdot\frac{V^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\check{\rho})-V^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\check{\rho})}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2H}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}(\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})=1),

where in the last equation we use

(
b
)

and
Lemma
5
. Similarly, for
\psi\in\Psi
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})}\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V(\check{\rho};\check{M})\right|\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2H}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})}(\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})=0).

Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt\qquad\sup_{\psi\in\Psi,i\in\{1,2\}}\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})}\left[\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V(\check{\rho};\check{M})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}\left[\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V(\check{\rho};\check{M})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{E}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})}\left[\left|\widehat{V}(D_{1:H-1,n})-V(\check{\rho};\check{M})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{4H}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})}(\delta(D_{1:H-1,n})=1)+\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{D_{1:H-1,n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})}(\delta(D_{,n})=0)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\left(i\right)}{\geq{}}\frac{\varepsilon}{4H}\cdot\left(1-D_{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}),\quad\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot;\check{\mu}_{\psi},\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\left(ii\right)}{\geq{}}\frac{\varepsilon}{4H}\cdot\left(1-D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\left(ii\right)}{\geq{}}\frac{\varepsilon}{4H}\cdot\left(1-\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\right),

where in the inequality
\left(i\right)
we use
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})+\mathbb{Q}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\geq 1-D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
for any event
\mathcal{E}
, in the inequality
\left(ii\right)
we use
(
23
)
, and in the inequality
\left(iii\right)
we use
(
24
)
and
Lemma
9
.

\blacksquare

Hence we only need to upper bound the TV distance between
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
, which is proved in the following lemma.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose for every
\phi\in\Psi
, we have

|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|\gtrsim\frac{|\phi|^{3}H^{8}\cdot\sup_{h\in H}|\Phi_{h}|\cdot\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|\cdot\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}}.

If
n\leq\frac{H}{8\varepsilon}\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)
, then we have

\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Proof of
Lemma
11
.

At a high level, the proof contains three steps:

(i)

We first define intermediate distributions
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
over tuples
({x},{a},{r},{x}^{\prime})
and observe via Triangle inequality that

\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}).

The final bound follows by showing that
\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
for all
i\in\{1,2\}
.

(ii)

Note that
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
is a distribution over tuples
({x},{a},{r},{x}^{\prime})
where the instantaneous reward
{r}\sim\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}(r\mid{x},{a})
. We first simplify our objective a bit by converting
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
to
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
, where
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
is a distribution over tuples
({x},{a},{r},{x}^{\prime})
where
{r}\sim\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}({r}\mid{x},{a})
where
\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
is the reward function in
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
. Another application of Triangle inequality implies that,

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}).

Bounding the first term above is straightforward.

(iii)

We finally bound the term
D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
for each
h\in[H-1]
by delving further into the structure of the MDPs and the underlying data distribution in
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
. Most of the proof will be spend on bound.

Part-
\left(i\right)
: Construction of
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
.

We first define additional notation. Let the distribution
\nu\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{X})
such that for any
h\in[H-1]
,
\phi\in\Phi_{h},\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}
and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi),{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\frac{\mu({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)||\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|},

(25)

Additionally, we define a reward distribution
\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}\in\Delta(\{-1,1\})
such that

\displaystyle\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(\cdot\mid{x},{a})=\begin{cases}\delta_{1}(\cdot)&\text{if}\quad{x}={u}_{h+1},\\
\delta_{-1}(\cdot)&\text{if}\quad{x}={v}_{h+1},\\
\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}(\cdot)+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{-1}(\cdot)&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(26)

where we use
\delta_{t}(\cdot)
denote the density of delta-distribution at
t
. Given
\nu
and
\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
above, we define
\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}\times\{-1,1\}\times\mathcal{X}_{h+1})
as

\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(({x},{a},r,{x}^{\prime}))\vcentcolon={}\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(r\mid{x},{a}),

(27)

and set
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}=(\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})^{\otimes n}
.

As a sanity check, note that

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\sum_{({x},{a},r,{x}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}\times\{-1,1\}\times\mathcal{X}_{h+1}}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(({x},{a},r,{x}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{X}_{h+1}}\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}^{\prime}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}}\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=1,

and thus
\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
is a valid distribution; the above also implies that
\nu_{h}
(defined above) is a valid distribution. Furthermore, while the above definition is based in
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
, we could have also defined
\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
using
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
and would have ended up with the same distribution since
\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\mu({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})
for any
\phi\in\Phi
,
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
due to
Lemma
4
-

(
c
)

.

Given
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
, using Triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)})\leq\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})+\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}).

(28)

In the next part, we prove that

\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. The proof for
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
follows similarly, and combining the two bound gives the desired statement.

Part-
\left(ii\right)
: Construction of
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and bounding
D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})
.

First recall that from
(
23
)
, we can write

\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n})=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\prod_{i\in[n]}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(r_{i}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{T}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i}^{\prime}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i}),

where
\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
is given by

\displaystyle\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\cdot\mid{x},{a})=\begin{cases}\delta_{1}(\cdot)&\text{if}\quad{x}={u}_{h+1},\\
\delta_{-1}(\cdot)&\text{if}\quad{x}={v}_{h+1},\\
R^{\star}&\text{if}\quad{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi)\text{ for }\phi\in{\mathcal{I}},a={\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}),\\
\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}(\cdot)+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{-1}(\cdot)&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(29)

where we use
R^{\star}
to denote

R^{\star}=\frac{\delta_{1}(\cdot)+\delta_{-1}(\cdot)}{2}+\frac{\delta_{1}(\cdot)-\delta_{-1}(\cdot)}{2}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{2H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h^{*}}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}.

We next define the distribution
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
similar to
{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
, but where we use
\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
(given in
(
26
)
) instead of
\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
to remove the dependence on the rewards on
i
. In particular, we define

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n})=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\prod_{i\in[n]}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(r_{i}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{T}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i}^{\prime}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i}).

(30)

If we denote

\check{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{h}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\vcentcolon={}\mathbb{E}[\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{h}(\cdot\mid{x},{a})]\quad\text{and}\quad\check{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}_{h}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\vcentcolon={}\mathbb{E}[\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}_{h}(\cdot\mid{x},{a})].

Note that when
n\leq\frac{H\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)}{2\varepsilon}
, using the above definitions, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121pt\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{h\in[H-1]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\\
\in(\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}\times\{0,1\}\times\mathcal{X}_{h+1})^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\left(\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\left(\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\left(i\right)}{\leq{}}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{h\in[H-1]}\sum_{\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}\in(\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A})^{n}}\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\sum_{i\in[n]}|\check{r}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})-\check{r}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})|\prod_{i\in[n]}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})

\displaystyle\overset{\left(ii\right)}{=}\frac{1}{2|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\sum_{i\in[n]}\sum_{h\in[H-1]}\sum_{({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\in\mathcal{X}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})|\check{r}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})-\check{r}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})|

\displaystyle\overset{\left(iii\right)}{\leq{}}\frac{1}{2|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\sum_{i\in[n]}\sum_{h\in[H-1]}\sum_{{x}_{i}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x}_{i},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}_{i}))\frac{\varepsilon\mathbb{I}\{\zeta(\psi({x}_{i}))\in{\mathcal{I}})\}}{2H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}

\displaystyle=\frac{n\varepsilon}{4H\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}\sum_{{z}:\zeta({z})\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mu({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})),

where inequality
\left(i\right)
follows from Triangle Inequality, inequality
\left(ii\right)
follows by rearranging the terms and using the fact that
\sum_{x_{i},a_{i}}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}(x_{i},a_{i})=1
. The inequality
\left(iii\right)
is from plugging in the forms of
\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
\check{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
from
(
26
)
and
(
29
)
. Finally, the last line uses the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\sum_{{x}_{i}}\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x}_{i},{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}_{i}))\mathbb{I}\{\zeta(\psi({x}_{i}))\in{\mathcal{I}})\}=\sum_{{z}:\zeta({z})\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mu({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})),

from the definition of
\check{\mu}
. Next, using the definition of
\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)
in
Definition
3.1
, and recalling that
{\mathcal{I}}
is the maximizer aggregation in
Definition
3.1
, we get that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)=\frac{\sum_{\phi\in{\mathcal{I}}}d_{h}^{\bar{\pi}_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi;\bar{M})}{\sum_{{z}:\zeta({z})\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mu({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}))},

which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n\varepsilon}{4H\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)}\leq\frac{1}{8},

where the last inequality holds since
n\leq\frac{H\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)}{2\varepsilon}
. Thus, using Triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})+\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})

(31)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}).

Part-
\left(iii\right)
: Bound on
D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})
.

First note that, from
Polyanskiy and Wu 2014
, we have

D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{D_{\chi^{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})}.

(32)

Using the form of
\chi^{2}
-divergence, we note that

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121ptD_{\chi^{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}}\left[\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n})}{\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n})}\right)^{2}\right]-1

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},r_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}}\left[\left(\frac{\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\prod_{i\in[n]}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(r_{i}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{T}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i}^{\prime}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})}{\prod_{i\in[n]}\nu_{h}({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(r_{i}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})}\right)^{2}\right]-1,

where the second line plugs in the definition of
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
in
(
30
)
and
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
in
(
27
)
. We next note that the terms
\check{R}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}(r_{i}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})
will cancel out in the ratio, thus implying that the expression is independent of
\{r_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Furthermore, from the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
in
(
27
)
, we note that sampling
\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
is same as sampling
\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\nu_{h}^{\otimes n}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121ptD_{\chi^{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\nu_{h}^{\otimes n}}\left[\left(\frac{\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\prod_{i\in[n]}\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{T}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i}^{\prime}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})}{\prod_{i\in[n]}\nu_{h}({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})}\right)^{2}\right]-1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\mathbb{E}_{\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\nu_{h}^{\otimes n}}\left[\prod_{i\in[n]}\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i}^{\prime}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}_{i}^{\prime}\mid{x}_{i},{a}_{i})}{\nu_{h}({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})^{2}}\right]-1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\left(\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\sim\nu_{h}}\left[\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})^{2}}\right]}_{\vcentcolon={}\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}\right)^{n}-1,

(33)

where the second equality follows by expanding the square and rearranging the product. Finally, the last line exchange the expectation and the product by using the fact that tuples
\{({x}_{i},{a}_{i},{x}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. sampled from
\nu_{h}
. In the following, we will complete the proof by giving bounds on the terms
\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
under various conditions on
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
. However, we need additional notation before we proceed:

\bullet

Given any
h\in[H-1]
,
\phi\in\Phi_{h}
,
{z},{z}^{\prime}\in\phi
, and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi
, we define

\displaystyle\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\vcentcolon={}\frac{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})|}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}

(34)

to denote the fraction of repeated observations between
{z}
and
{z}^{\prime}
amongst all the observations that correspond to aggregation
\phi.

\bullet

Let
\xi=\nicefrac{{1}}{{(64H^{2}n)}}\in(0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}})
. For any
h\in[H-1]
,
\phi\in\Phi_{h}
, and
{z}_{1},{z}_{2},\in\phi
, we define

\displaystyle\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})

\displaystyle=\left\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in\Psi^{2}\mid{}\theta({\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})\leq\frac{1+\xi}{|\phi|^{2}}\right\}

(35)

to denote the set of all pairs
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
for which the corresponding ratios
\theta({\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
are small.

\bullet

Finally, we define the set

\displaystyle\Gamma(\xi)=\bigcap_{h\in[H-1],\phi\in\Phi_{h}\cup\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h}\}}\left(\bigcap_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2},\in\phi}\Gamma(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\right).

(36)

We now have all the necessary notation to proceed with the proof. We split the terms
\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
appearing in
(
33
)
under two separate scenarios:

\bullet

Case 1:
\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in\Gamma(\xi)
. Here,
Lemma
12
(below) implies that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq(1+\xi)^{2}.

\bullet

Case 2:
\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\notin\Gamma(\xi)
. Here,
Lemma
13
(below) implies that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4}.

Combining the two above in
(
33
)
, we get that

\displaystyle D_{\chi^{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in\Gamma(\xi)\}(1+\xi)^{2n}+\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma(\xi)\}\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4n}\right)\right]-1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\left[\left(1+2n\xi+\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma(\xi)\}\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4n}\right)\right]-1

\displaystyle=2\xi n+\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4n}\cdot\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma(\xi)\},

where the second line uses the fact that
(1+\xi)^{n}\leq 1+2\xi n
for any
\xi\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}
. Next, we notice that for
\phi\in\cup_{h=1}^{H}\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h}\}
and
{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi
, since
|\phi|=1
, we always have
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in\Gamma(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})
. Therefore, we have

\Gamma(\xi)=\bigcap_{h\in[H-1],\phi\in\Phi_{h}}\left(\bigcap_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2},\in\phi}\Gamma(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\right).

Lemma
14
gives that for any
h\in H,\phi\in\Phi_{h}
and
{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\Phi
, we always have

\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\}\leq e^{-2\xi^{2}\frac{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}{|\phi|^{3}}}\leq\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\}\leq e^{-2\xi^{2}\inf_{\phi\in\Phi}\frac{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}{|\phi|^{3}}},

which indicates that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma(\xi)\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h\in[H-1],\phi\in\Phi_{h}}\sum_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi}\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\}

\displaystyle\leq H\cdot\sup_{h\in H}|\Phi_{h}|\cdot\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{2}\cdot e^{-2\xi^{2}\inf_{\phi\in\Phi}\frac{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}{\phi|^{3}}}

This suggests that

D_{\chi^{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})\leq 2\xi n+H\cdot\sup_{h\in H}|\Phi_{h}|\cdot\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4n+2}\cdot e^{-2\xi^{2}\inf_{\phi\in\Phi}\frac{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}{|\phi|^{3}}}.

Finally, when

|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|\geq c_{0}\cdot n^{3}|\phi|^{3}H^{5}\cdot\sup_{h\in H}|\Phi_{h}|\cdot\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|\qquad\forall\phi\in\Phi

for some sufficiently large constant
c_{0}
, with choice
\xi=\nicefrac{{1}}{{(64H^{2}n)}}
we will have

\displaystyle D_{\chi^{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\|\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})\leq\frac{1}{16H^{2}}.

Hence when

|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|\gtrsim\frac{|\phi|^{3}H^{8}\cdot\sup_{h\in H}|\Phi_{h}|\cdot\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\quad\text{and}\quad n\leq\frac{H}{8\varepsilon}\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)

according to
(
32
)
we have

D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})\leq\frac{1}{8H}.

According to
(
31
)
, we have

\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})\leq\frac{1}{8}+H\cdot\frac{1}{8H}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Similarly, we can also prove that

\sum_{h\in[H-1]}D_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\mathbb{P}_{h,n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})\cdot\frac{1}{4}.

Plugging in these two in
(
28
)
, we get the desired bound.

\blacksquare

Lemma 12
.

We have the following property for
\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
defined in
(
33
)
: For any
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Gamma(\xi)
, we have

\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq(1+\xi)^{2}.

Proof of
Lemma
12
.

From
(
33
)
, recall that

\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})=\mathbb{E}_{({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\sim\nu_{h}}\left[\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})^{2}}\right].

According to our construction of
\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}
in
Appendix
C.4.1
, for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
, we have

\check{\mu}_{h,\psi}({x},{a})=\frac{\mu_{h}({z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}({z})|}=\mu_{h}({z},{a})\cdot\frac{|\phi|}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}.

Additionally, according to our construction of
\check{T}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})
, for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}({z})
and
{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}({z}^{\prime})
, we have

\check{T}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})=\frac{T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|}=T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})\cdot\frac{|\phi^{\prime}|}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|}.

This implies that for any
\phi\in\Phi_{h},\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}^{\prime}
, and
{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2}),{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1}^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2}^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})

\displaystyle=\frac{|\phi|^{2}}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|^{2}}\cdot\frac{|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|^{2}}\cdot\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})\mu_{h}({z}_{2},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}\mid{z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{2}\mid{z}_{2},{a}).

(37)

And the definition of
\theta
in
(
34
)
gives that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})|

\displaystyle=\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\cdot|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|

\displaystyle|\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1}^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\theta_{h+1}(\phi^{\prime},{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\cdot|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|

Hence we can write

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt\mathbb{E}_{({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\sim\nu_{h}}\left[\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\sum_{{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}}\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\in\Phi_{h}\\
\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{R}(a,\phi,\phi^{\prime}),

(38)

where we defined the quantity

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}(a,\phi,\phi^{\prime})\vcentcolon={}\sum_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi)}\sum_{{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})}\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\frac{\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)||\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|}}.

We next bound the terms
\mathfrak{R}(a,\phi,\phi^{\prime})
. Notice that

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121pt\mathfrak{R}(a,\phi,\phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\left(i\right)}{=}\sum_{{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi)}\sum_{{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})}\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\frac{\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)||\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|}}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(ii\right)}{=}\sum_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})\mu_{h}({z}_{2},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}\mid{z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{2}\mid{z}_{2},{a})

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\cdot\frac{\frac{|\phi|^{2}}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|^{2}}\cdot\frac{|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|^{2}}\cdot|\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1}^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2}^{\prime})||\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})|}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\frac{\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)||\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|}}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(iii\right)}{=}\sum_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})\mu_{h}({z}_{2},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}\mid{z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{2}\mid{z}_{2},{a})

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\frac{\frac{|\phi|^{2}}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|^{2}}\cdot\frac{|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|^{2}}\cdot\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\cdot\theta_{h+1}(\phi^{\prime},{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\cdot|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\frac{\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)||\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime})|}}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(iv\right)}{=}\sum_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}|\phi|^{2}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}\cdot\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})\mu_{h}({z}_{2},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}\mid{z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{2}\mid{z}_{2},{a})

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\frac{\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\cdot\theta_{h+1}(\phi^{\prime},{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})},

(39)

where in
\left(i\right)
we use the exact form of
\nu_{h}
defined in
(
25
)
, in
\left(ii\right)
we group those
{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})
and
{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1}^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2}^{\prime})
together (because the summand gives the same value) and use
(
37
)
, in
\left(iii\right)
we use the definition of
