\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
in
(
34
)
, and
\left(iv\right)
is just algebraic calculation.

Next, when
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in\Gamma(\xi)
, according to the definition of
\gamma(\xi)
in
(
36
)
, we have

(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\quad\forall h\in[H-1],\phi\in\Phi_{h},{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\psi,

which implies that

\displaystyle\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq\frac{1+\xi}{|\phi|^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\theta_{h+1}(\phi^{\prime},{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq\frac{1+\xi}{|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}}.

(40)

Bringing this back to
(
39
)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121pt\mathfrak{R}(a,\phi,\phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\left(i\right)}{\leq{}}(1+\xi)^{2}\cdot\sum_{{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}_{1}^{\prime},{z}_{2}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\frac{\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})\mu_{h}({z}_{2},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{2}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{2},{a})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(ii\right)}{=}(1+\xi)^{2}\cdot\sum_{{z}_{1}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}_{1}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{1},{a}),

where in
\left(i\right)
we use
(
40
)
, and
\left(ii\right)
is by straightforward algebraic calculations. Bringing this back to
(
38
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt\mathbb{E}_{({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\sim\nu_{h}}\left[\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\in\Phi_{h}\\
\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{R}(a,\phi,\phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\left(iii\right)}{\leq{}}(1+\xi)^{2}\sum_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})=(1+\xi)^{2},

where in
\left(iii\right)
we use the fact that
\mu_{h}(\cdot)\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z}_{h}\times\mathcal{A})
and
T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\cdot\mid{z},{a})\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z}_{h+1})
for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
and
{a}\in\mathcal{A}
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 13
.

For any
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\not\in\Gamma(\xi)
, the term
\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
satisfies

\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4}.

Proof of
Lemma
13
.

For
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma(\xi)
, since for any
h\in[H-1]
,
\phi\in\Phi_{h}\cup\{{u}_{h},{v}_{h}\}
and
{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\phi
, we always have

\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})\subset\mathcal{X}(\phi),

we have
\theta_{h}(\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2};\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq 1
. Bringing this back to
(
39
)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip-14.45377pt\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})\sim\nu_{h}}\left[\frac{\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{1}}({x},{a})\check{\mu}_{h,\psi_{2}}({x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})\check{T}_{\psi_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a})}{\nu_{h}({x},{a},{x}^{\prime})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}|\phi|^{2}\sup_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\sum_{{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\in\Phi_{h}\\
\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{z}_{2}\in\phi\\
{z}_{2}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})\mu_{h}({z}_{2},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{2}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{2},{a})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi}\sum_{{z}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}}\mu_{h}({z},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}^{\prime}\mid{z},{a})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}|\phi|^{2}\sup_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}\cdot\sum_{{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\in\Phi_{h}\\
\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h+1}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{z}_{1}\in\phi\\
{z}_{1}^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{1},{a})\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}|\phi|^{2}\sup_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}\\
{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\mu_{h}({z}_{1},{a})T^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}({z}_{1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{1},{a})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}|\phi|^{2}\sup_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}\cup\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}.

Further notice that for any
\phi^{\prime}\in\{{u}_{h+1},{v}_{h+1}\}
, we have
|\phi^{\prime}|\leq 1\leq\sup_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}}|\phi^{\prime}|
, which indicates that
\mathfrak{B}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\leq\sup_{\phi\in\Phi_{h}}|\phi|^{2}\cdot\sup_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{h}}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}\leq\sup_{\phi\in\Phi}|\phi|^{4}
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 14
.

For any
h\in[H-1],\phi\in\Phi_{h}
and
{z}_{1},{z}_{2}\in\Phi
and
\xi\in(0,1)
, we have

\frac{1}{|\Psi|^{2}}\sum_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\Psi}\mathbb{I}\{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\not\in\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\}\leq e^{-2\xi^{2}\frac{|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|}{|\phi|^{3}}},

where
\Gamma_{h}(\xi;\phi,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})
is defined in
(
35
)

Proof of
Lemma
14
.

We denote
S=|\mathcal{X}(\phi)|
. Without loss of generality, we assume
\mathcal{X}(\phi)=[S]=\{1,2,\cdots,S\}
. Since
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are samples i.i.d. according to
\text{Unif}(\Psi)
, without loss of generality we assume
\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{1}}({z}_{1})=[\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}]
. And to prove this lemma, we only need to verify that when
\psi_{2}\sim\mathrm{Uniform}(\Psi)
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})\cap\left[\frac{S}{|\phi|}\right]\right|\geq\frac{S(1+\xi)}{|\phi|^{2}}\right)\leq e^{-2\xi^{2}\frac{S}{|\phi|^{3}}}.

(41)

Next, we notice that sampling
\psi_{2}\sim\mathrm{Uniform}(\Psi)
is equivalent of sampling
\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})
uniformly from all subsets of
[S]
with exact
\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}
elements. Hence we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{X}_{\psi_{2}}({z}_{2})\cap\left[\frac{S}{|\phi|}\right]\right|\geq\frac{S(1+\xi)}{|\phi|^{2}}\right)=\sum_{t\geq\nicefrac{{S(1+\xi)}}{{|\phi|^{2}}}}\frac{\binom{\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}}{t}\binom{S-\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}}{\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}-t}}{\binom{S}{\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}}}.

We further notice that according to Lemma D.7 in
Foster et al. 2022
(also in
Hoeffding 1994
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq\nicefrac{{S(1+\xi)}}{{|\phi|^{2}}}}\frac{\binom{\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}}{t}\binom{S-\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}}{\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}-t}}{\binom{S}{\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}}}}=\mathbb{P}\left[X\geq\left(\frac{K}{N}+\frac{\xi}{|\phi|}\right)N^{\prime}\right]\leq e^{-2\xi^{2}\frac{S}{|\phi|^{3}}},

where
X\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(K,N,N^{\prime})
with
K=N^{\prime}=\nicefrac{{S}}{{|\phi|}},N=S
. This verifies
(
41
)
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 15

(Lemma D.7 in
Foster et al. 2022
)
.

Let
X\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(K,N,N^{\prime})
and define
p=K/N
. Then for any
0<\varepsilon<pN^{\prime}
, we have

\mathbb{P}[X\geq(p+\varepsilon)N^{\prime}]\leq\exp(-2\varepsilon^{2}N^{\prime}).

C.5.2
Proof of
Theorem
C.1

Proof of
Theorem
C.1
.

Given the original
MDP

M
and distribution
\mu
, we construct family
\mathfrak{G}
of
OPE
problems in
Appendix
C.4.2
. First of all,
Lemma
8
indicates that the size of the function class
\mathcal{F}
of these
OPE
problems in
\mathfrak{G}
is
2
. Next, bringing
Lemma
11
into
Lemma
10
and noticing that
(
21
)
satisfies the condition of
Lemma
11
, we have that any algorithm which takes
D_{h,n}
for
h\in[H-1]
must induce error at least
\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8H}}
in one case within
\mathfrak{G}
.

Additionally,
Corollary
3
indicates that the standard concentrability coefficient of instances in
\mathfrak{G}
is equal to to the standard concentrability coefficient of
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mu^{\prime})
or
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\mu^{\prime})
. Additionally,
Corollary
1
indicates that
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\mu^{\prime})
or
\mathsf{C}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\mu^{\prime})
is no more than
6\mathsf{C}(M,\mu,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}})
. This verifies the second condition in
Theorem
C.1
.

\blacksquare

Appendix D
Missing Details from
Section
3.1

and
Section
4.1

In this subsection, we will prove
Proposition
1

and
Theorem
4.1
.

D.1
Proof of
Proposition
1

The following lemmas state some properties of the
MDP
defined in
Example
1
.
Proposition
1
is a direct corollary of
Lemma
19
.

Lemma 16
.

For
MDP

M
, and policy
{\pi_{b}}
defined above, we have

d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)\geq\frac{1}{2^{h+2}},\quad d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]};M)\geq\frac{1}{2^{h+2}H},\quad\text{and}\quad d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]};M)\geq\frac{1}{4},\quad\forall 1\leq h\leq H.

Proof of
Lemma
16
.

Under policy
{\pi_{b}}({z})=\frac{1}{H^{2}}\delta_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}(\cdot)+\frac{H^{2}-1}{H^{2}}\delta_{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}(\cdot)
, the transition satisfies

\displaystyle T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))\geq\frac{H^{2}-1}{H^{2}}T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\mathfrak{a}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{H^{2}-1}{2H^{2}},

\displaystyle T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))\geq\frac{H^{2}-1}{H^{2}}T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},\mathfrak{a}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{H^{2}-1}{2H^{2}},\penalty\ \text{and,}

\displaystyle\quad T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]}))\geq\frac{H^{2}-1}{H^{2}}T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]},\mathfrak{a}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{H^{2}-1}{H^{2}}.

Hence according to the choice initial distribution
\rho(\cdot)
, we have for all
1\leq h\leq H
,

\displaystyle d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{H-1}{2H}\cdot\left(\frac{H^{2}-1}{2H^{2}}\right)^{h-1}\geq\frac{1}{2^{h+2}},

\displaystyle\quad d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]};M)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2H}\cdot\left(\frac{H^{2}-1}{2H^{2}}\right)^{h-1}\geq\frac{1}{2^{h+2}H},\penalty\ \text{and,}

\displaystyle\quad d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[3]};M)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{H^{2}-1}{H^{2}}\right)^{h-1}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

\blacksquare

Lemma 17
.

For
MDP

M
and policy
{\pi_{b}}
defined above, we have

\frac{d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}{d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}\geq\frac{H-1}{3}.

Proof of
Lemma
17
.

First we can write the dynamic programming formula for
d^{\pi}_{h}
:

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})

\displaystyle=d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))+d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})),

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})

\displaystyle=d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))+d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})).

According to our choice of
{\pi_{b}}
, we have

\displaystyle T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))=\frac{H^{2}-1}{2H^{2}},

\displaystyle\quad T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))=\frac{1}{H^{2}},

\displaystyle T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))=0,

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))=\frac{H^{2}-1}{2H^{2}},

which indicates that

\displaystyle\frac{d_{h+1}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}{d_{h+1}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}

\displaystyle=\frac{T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))}{T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))}+\frac{T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))}{T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))}\cdot\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}\leq\frac{2}{H^{2}-1}+\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}.

Additionally, after noticing that
\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}=\frac{\rho({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}{\rho({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}=\frac{1}{H-1}
, we have for all
1\leq h\leq H
,

\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})}\leq\frac{1}{H-1}+(h-1)\cdot\frac{2}{H^{2}-1}\leq\frac{1}{H-1}+\frac{2(H-1)}{H^{2}-1}\leq\frac{3}{H-1}.

\blacksquare

Lemma 18
.

For any policy
\pi
, and the
MDP

M
defined above, we have

d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})\leq\frac{1}{2^{h-1}}\quad\forall h\in[H].

Proof of
Lemma
18
.
First we can write the dynamic programming formula for
d^{\pi}_{h}
:

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})

\displaystyle=d^{\pi}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))+d^{\pi}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))

\displaystyle d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})

\displaystyle=d^{\pi}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))+d^{\pi}_{h}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})).

We let
\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})=p_{1}\delta_{a_{1}}(\cdot)+(1-p_{1})\delta_{a_{2}}(\cdot)
, and
\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})=p_{2}\delta_{a_{1}}(\cdot)+(1-p_{2})\delta_{a_{2}}(\cdot)
. Then we have

\displaystyle T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))=\frac{1}{2}(1-p_{1}),

\displaystyle\quad T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}))=p_{1},

\displaystyle T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))=0,

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad T({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},\pi({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}))=\frac{1}{2}(1-p_{2}).

This implies that

\displaystyle 2d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+d_{h+1}^{\pi}({z}_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})

\displaystyle=d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})\cdot\left(1-p_{1}p_{1}\right)+d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})\cdot\left(2\cdot 0+\frac{1}{2}(1-p_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\left(2d^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+d^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})\right).

Further noticing that
2d^{\pi}({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+d^{\pi}({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})=2\rho({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+\rho({z}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})\leq 1
, we obtain that for any
h\in[H]
,

2d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})\leq\frac{1}{2^{h-1}}.

Therefore, for any
h\in[H]
, we have

d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})+d_{h}^{\pi}({z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})\leq\frac{1}{2^{h-1}}.

\blacksquare

Lemma 19
.

For the
MDP

M
and policy
{\pi_{b}}
defined above, the concentrability coefficient of all policies with respect to
d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}(\cdot;M)
is upper bounded as

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{h}\max_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h},{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z},{a};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z},{a};M)}\leq 8H^{3},

where
\Pi
is the class of all policies. However, for
\varepsilon\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{15}}
, the aggregated concentrability coefficient is lower bounded as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}(\cdot;M))\geq 2^{H-7}.

Proof of
Lemma
19
.

For our choice of
{\pi_{b}}
,

d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z},{a};M)\geq\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z};M)}{H^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad d_{h}^{\pi}({z},{a};M)\leq\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{H^{2}}\quad\forall{z}\in\mathcal{Z},{a}\in\mathcal{A},

which implies that

\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z},{a};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z},{a};M)}\leq H^{2}\cdot\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z};M)}\qquad\forall{z}\in\mathcal{Z}.

Hence,
Lemma
16
and
Lemma
18
give that for any policy
\pi\in\Pi,1\leq h\leq H
and
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
,

\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z};M)}\leq\max\left\{4,\frac{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2^{h-1}}}}{\nicefrac{{1}}{{(2^{h+2}H)}}}\right\}=8H.

This implies that

\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{h}\max_{{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h},{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{d_{h}^{\pi}({z},{a};M)}{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z},{a};M)}\leq 8H^{3}.

As for the lower bound of
\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,d^{\pi_{b}}(\cdot;M))
, first we notice that according to
(
3
)
, our choice of
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z})=\delta_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}(\cdot)
and
Lemma
17
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{T}(\phi_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid\phi_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{{z}_{h}\in\phi_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{z}_{h+1}^{\prime}\in\phi_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}}d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h},\mathfrak{a}_{1};M)T({z}_{h+1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{h},\mathfrak{a}_{1})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}}d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z},\mathfrak{a}_{1};M)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{{z}_{h+1}^{\prime}\in\phi_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}}d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h},\mathfrak{a}_{1};M)T({z}_{h+1}^{\prime}\mid{z}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\mathfrak{a}_{1})}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}}d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h},\mathfrak{a}_{1};M)}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h},\mathfrak{a}_{1};M)}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}}d_{h}^{\pi_{b}}({z}_{h},\mathfrak{a}_{1};M)}=\frac{H-1}{H-1+3}=\frac{H-1}{H+2}.

This implies that
\bar{d}_{H}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)
satisfies for
H\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\bar{d}_{H}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)\geq\bar{d}_{1}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)\prod_{h=1}^{H-1}\bar{T}(\phi_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}\mid\phi_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},\pi)\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{H-1}{H+2}\right)^{H-1}\geq\frac{1}{2e^{3}}\geq\frac{1}{41}.

Hence when
\varepsilon\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{15}}
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\Phi,\mu)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\bar{d}_{H}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)}{\sum_{{z}\in\phi_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}}d^{\pi_{b}}_{H}({z},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z});M)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{d}_{H}^{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\phi_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]};M)}{d^{\pi_{b}}_{H}({z}_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]});M)+d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({z}_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({z}_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]});M)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nicefrac{{1}}{{41}}}{\nicefrac{{1}}{{(2^{H-1})}}}

\displaystyle\geq 2^{H-7},

where in the second inequality we adopt
Lemma
18
with
\pi={\pi_{b}}
.

\blacksquare

D.2
Proof of
Theorem
4.1

In this section, we will prove the following stronger results of
Theorem
4.1
.

Theorem D.1
.

There exists a class
\mathfrak{G}
of realizable
OPE
problems such that for every
OPE
problem
\mathfrak{g}=\textsc{OPE}\left(M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},{\pi_{b}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})},\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}\right)
in
\mathfrak{G}
,

(a)

The function class
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
satisfies
\lvert\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}\rvert=2
and

(Q^{{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}),W^{{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;d^{{\pi_{b}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})});M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}))\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}.

(b)

The concentrability coefficient of all policies with respect to
d_{h}^{{\pi_{b}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})})
is upper bounded by
384H^{3}
.

Furthermore, any offline policy evaluation algorithm that guarantees to estimate the value of
{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
in the
MDP

M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
up to precision
\varepsilon
, in expectation, for every
OPE
problem
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathfrak{G}
must use

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Omega}\left(\frac{H2^{H}}{\varepsilon}\right)

admissible samples collected according to policy
{\pi_{b}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
under MDP
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
. Here one admissible sample consists of
({x}_{h},{a}_{h},r_{h},{x}_{h+1}^{\prime})
for all
h\in[H-1]
, where
({x}_{h},{a}_{h})\sim d_{h}^{{\pi_{b}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}}(\cdot;M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})})
, and
r_{h},{x}_{h+1}^{\prime}
are collected according to the transition model and reward function of
M^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Proof of
Theorem
D.1
.

We consider the class of offline RL problems constructed in
Appendix
C.4.2
:
\mathfrak{G}=\bigcup_{\psi\in\Psi}\{\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}_{\psi},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}_{\psi}\}
. According to the construction, the sampling distributions over rich observations in
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}_{\psi}
or
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}_{\psi}
are
\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x},{a})=\nicefrac{{\mu(\psi({z}),{a})}}{{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})|}}
. And
Theorem
C.1
indicates that for
\varepsilon\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{41}}
, for any algorithm using less than

\frac{H\bar{\mathsf{C}}_{\varepsilon}(M,\phi,\mu)}{8\varepsilon}\geq 2^{H-10}\frac{H}{\varepsilon}

samples, there must exist some
\psi\in\Phi
such that if the samples are according to
\check{\mu}_{\psi}
, the algorithm will have error greater than
\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8H}}
in
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}_{\psi}
or
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}_{\psi}
with probability at least
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
.

According to
Lemma
4

(
d
)

,
we have
d^{\pi_{b}}({z};M^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})\leq d^{\pi_{b}}({z};M)
for any
{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Hence we obtain that for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}\backslash\{{u},{v},{w}\}_{h=1}^{H}
,

d^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{b}}}}({x};\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})=\frac{d^{\pi_{b}}(\psi({x});M)}{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})|}\leq\frac{d^{\pi_{b}}(\psi({x});M)}{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})|}.

This also implies

d^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{b}}}}({x},{a};\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]})=d^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{b}}}}({x};\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}){\pi_{b}}({a}\mid{x})\leq\frac{d^{\pi_{b}}(\psi({x});M){\pi_{b}}({a}\mid{x})}{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})|}=\frac{d^{\pi_{b}}(\psi({x}),{a};M)}{|\mathcal{X}_{\psi}({z})|}=\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x},{a}).

Similarly, we can also obtain that

d^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{b}}}}({x},{a};\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]})\leq\check{\mu}_{\psi}({x},{a}).

Hence, for any algorithm using less than
\nicefrac{{2^{H-10}H^{3}}}{{\varepsilon}}
number of samples, there must exists some
\check{M}\in\{\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle[1]}:\psi\in\Psi\}\cup\{\check{M}_{\psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle[2]}:\psi\in\Psi\}
such that if the samples are according to
d^{{\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{b}}}}(\cdot\mid\check{M})
, the estimation error is at least
\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{H}}
.

Finally,
Lemma
19
indicates that the concentrability coefficient of
M
is bounded by
8H^{3}
. Hence according to
Corollary
2
and
Lemma
7
we have for any such
\check{M}
, the concentrability coefficient is upper bounded by
384H^{3}
, which proves condition

(
b
)

. And similar to the proof of
Theorem
C.1
, we can also verify condition

(
a
)

.

\blacksquare

E.1
Algorithms

In this subsection, we provide algorithms mentioned in
Section
4.2
.
Algorithm
1
will transform a hard-case
OPE
problem for admissible data into a hard-case
OPE
problem for trajectory data.
Algorithm
2
is used in the proof of lower bound with trajectory data, where the algorithm can transform admissible data collected according to
M
(MDP in the hard-case
OPE
problem of admissible data) into trajectory data of
\widetilde{M}
(MDP in the hard-case
OPE
problem of trajectory data). And finally,
Algorithm
3
shows how to transform an algorithm for
OPE
with trajectory data to an algorithm for
OPE
with admissible data.

1:

Offline policy evaluation problem
\textsc{OPE}(M,{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}},{\pi_{b}},\mathcal{F})
with
M=\textsc{MDP}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},T,r,H,\rho)
, and parameter
K\geq 1
.

2:

Define
{\pi_{c}}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{A}
to be an arbitrary mapping which satisfies
{\pi_{c}}({x})\neq{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x})
for all
{x}\in\mathcal{X}
.

3:

/*
Construct
\widetilde{M}=\textsc{MDP}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{A},\widetilde{T},\widetilde{r},\widetilde{H},\widetilde{\rho})
*/

4:

Horizon:

\widetilde{H}=K(H-1)+1
.

5:

State space:
Let
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup_{l\in[\widetilde{H}]}\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{l}
, where the state space
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{l}=\{({x},k):{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h},k\in[K]\}

l=(h-1)K+k
, for
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]
.

6:

Initial distribution:
Let
\widetilde{\rho}(({x},1))=\rho({x})
for
{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{1}
.

7:

Transition model:
Define the transition matrix
\widetilde{T}:\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}\times\mathcal{A}\mapsto\Delta(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})
such that

\bullet

When
k\leq K-1
: For any
h\in[H]
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
, and
{x},{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
, let

\widetilde{T}\left(({x}^{\prime},k+1)\mid{}({x},k),{a}\right)=\begin{cases}\mathbb{I}({x}^{\prime}={x})&\text{if}\quad a={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}),\\
d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({x}^{\prime};M)&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

\bullet

When
k=K
: For any
h\in[H-1]
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
, and
{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}
, let

\widetilde{T}(({x}^{\prime},1)\mid({x},k),{a})=T({x}^{\prime}\mid{x},{a}).

8:

Reward function:
For any
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}(({x},k),{a})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if}\quad k\leq K-1,\\
r(s,a)&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

9:

/*
Construct evaluation policy
{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\mathrm{e}}}
*/

10:

Construct
{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\mathrm{e}}}
such that
{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\mathrm{e}}}(({x},k))={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x})
for all
{x}\in\mathcal{X}
and
k\in[K]
.

11:

/*
Construct offline policy
{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{b}}
*/

12:

Construct
{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{b}}
such that, for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}
and
k\in[K]
,

{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{b}}(({x},k))=\begin{cases}{\pi_{b}}({x})&\text{if}\quad k=K,\\
\frac{1}{2}{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x})+\frac{1}{2}{\pi_{c}}({x})&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

13:

/*
Construct state-action value function class
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
*/

14:

Construct
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\vcentcolon={}\{\widetilde{Q}:Q\in\mathcal{F}\}
, where
\widetilde{Q}:\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}\times\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}(({x}_{h},k),{a})=\begin{cases}Q({x}_{h},{a})&\text{if}\quad k=K,\\
Q({x}_{h},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}_{h}))&\text{if}\quad k<K,{a}={\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}_{h}),\\
\sum_{{x}_{h}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}d^{\pi_{b}}_{h}({x}_{h}^{\prime};M)Q({x}_{h}^{\prime},{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}_{h}^{\prime}))&\text{if}\quad k<K,{a}\neq{\pi_{\mathrm{e}}}({x}_{h}).\end{cases}

(42)

for any
{x}\in\mathcal{X}
and
k\in[K]
.

15:

Return:
Offline policy evaluation problem
\textsc{OPE}(\widetilde{M},{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\mathrm{e}}},{{\widetilde{\pi}}_{b}},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})
.
